**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** The black tide...

*(Approach the marked location)*

**Dan Heng:** An "unspeakable" thing of "unknown origin"... What comes to your mind when we put these keywords together?

> *(Trailblazer)* A Stellaron?
> **Dan Heng:** That's right. If you think about the various characteristics of Amphoreus — an isolated world of eternal night being eroded by an unknown power... we cannot discount this possibility.

> *(Trailblazer)* An Aeon?
> **Dan Heng:** That's right. If you think about the various characteristics of Amphoreus — an isolated world of eternal night being eroded by an unknown power... we cannot discount this possibility.

> *(Trailblazer)* Uhh... a Leviathan? A cosmic mega octopus?
> **Dan Heng:** ...A hypothesis like that has never occurred to me, I must admit. But considering how the Leviathan hasn't been sighted in the universe for hundreds of Amber Eras, I think the possibility of it being the culprit is minuscule. Let's think about the various characteristics of Amphoreus instead. An isolated world of eternal night being eroded by an unknown power...

**Dan Heng:** This has been bothering me for a while now. Everything we have seen thus far suggests that Amphoreus wasn't always isolated from the rest of the universe. Be it the birth of the Titans or the encroaching black tide, none of these can be explained as a "natural progression" of a civilization.
**Dan Heng:** There must be other reasons for its current state of isolation. It's just... if the Cancer of All Worlds is truly the culprit, then we should have observed certain phenomena — explicit or otherwise — by now, but that isn't the case.

> *(Trailblazer)* The Voice of the Stellaron?
> **Dan Heng:** That's what I was thinking too. From past experience, the Stellaron within you should be able to resonate with other Stellarons, but we haven't encountered anything like that here.
> *(Trailblazer)* The Antimatter Legion?
> **Dan Heng:** That's what I was thinking too. From past experience, the Destruction has always been involved in some way when it comes to a world corrupted by a Stellaron.
> *(Trailblazer)* The Stellaron Hunters?
> **Dan Heng:** That's what I was thinking too. From past experience, Stellaron Hunters are certain to appear on a world with a Stellaron.

**Dan Heng:** There are several other unanswered questions, too. My gut feeling is that things here are not as simple as they seem... Furthermore, what happened in Penacony also proved that the appearance of a world may not necessarily be due to the influence of just one Path.

> *(Trailblazer)* No sweat! The Trailblaze can tackle even ten Paths if it must!
> **Dan Heng:** *sigh* It's good to be motivated at least.
> *(Trailblazer)* It could be an all-star cast.
> **Dan Heng:** Heh... In another sense, it's Penacony all over again.
> *(Trailblazer)* Who knows, maybe Akivili's behind it all.
> **Dan Heng:** ...Not even the History Fictionologists would dare to spin a tale like that.

**Dan Heng:** For now, let's just take things as they come. The black tide could be a good starting point. Since Nikador has fallen, those who were suppressed by them will no doubt begin to counterattack now. It seems our trailblazing expedition is growing in significance...

---

*Switching to Mydei's POV...*
*Mydei's people, the Kremnoans, seem to have other plans for the divine authority of Strife. Before overcoming the trial, he must first gain the trust of his people...*

### Part 87 — `databank/missions/chapter-02-light-slips.md`
**Context:** Mission 3: Grove, Wherefore Are the Wise Silent > Speak with Aglaea (Grove Mission)
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-02-light-slips__speak-with-aglaea-grove-mission`

**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** You're finally here, (Trailblazer).

**Aglaea:** Welcome, Cas. And you as well, Trailblazer from beyond the sky.

> *(Trailblazer)* You're up so early, Dan Heng!
> **Dan Heng:** You would've been, too, if you hadn't stayed up so late. But what matters is that you got a good rest.
> *(Trailblazer)* I didn't expect you to call me that.
> **Aglaea:** Your companion here kindly explained the meaning behind the name to me earlier. Through him, I've come to understand that you and your friends walk the paths of Kephale and Janus in places beyond the sky. Addressing you appropriately is the least I can do as a show of respect.
> *(Trailblazer)* The gift! Gimme the gift!

**Aglaea:** For all that has happened, it was never our intention to drag you two into the battle against Nikador and jeopardize your safety. To express our gratitude for your kind Trailblazing endeavors, we would like to present you with the highest honor of Okhema.

**Aglaea:** This is the Dew of Divine Blood, brewed from crops nourished by the blood of the Titans. It is said that only twelve bottles existed. The last three bottles are now being kept in the treasure vault of Okhema.

**Aglaea:** Back in the days of prosperity, cities fortunate enough to acquire it regarded it as one of the greatest treasures ever. Unsealing it during a ceremony held in honor of two cities' diplomatic ties was the highest form of respect one could show the other.

**Aglaea:** And now, in keeping with tradition, Okhema dedicates this drink to our esteemed guests from beyond the sky in appreciation of what you have done for us.

> *(Trailblazer)* My hand is trembling under the weight of this gift...
> *(Trailblazer)* I'm not of drinking age yet.
> **Dan Heng:** ...The Express has house rules about drinking, and even I'm not exempt to them.

**Dan Heng:** This precious gift isn't simply a token of gratitude though, is it, Lady Aglaea?

**Aglaea:** Very perceptive, Mr. Dan Heng. Your heroic deeds have proven that you come in good faith. With the Dew of Divine Blood as testament, we wish to forge an alliance with the Trailblazers.

**Dan Heng:** With all due respect... won't forging an alliance with the Chrysos Heirs embroil us in the conflicts within Okhema? I'm sure you can understand where our misgivings come from, Lady Aglaea.

> *(Trailblazer)* Right, we need to think about this first.
> *(Trailblazer)* Waaaah, my gift! My gift...
> **Dan Heng:** ...Wow, you seriously took a liking to the gift.

**Dan Heng:** Having said that, Lady Aglaea, please don't misunderstand, we will still do what we can to assist you in the Flame-Chase Journey. It's just... I've heard of the friction between the Chrysos Heirs and the Council of Okhema. As outsiders, we are wary of getting caught up in this conflict—

**Hyacine:** Wow, Grayie and Dannie have sharp eyes indeed! You'd make outstanding members of the Twilight Courtyard!

**Hyacine:** No need to worry, by the way. I can tell from Lady Aglaea's tone of voice and micro-expressions that she's being genuine. Not only are you guests from beyond the sky, but you've also done so much for Okhema. You're practically heroes to us all! Lady Aglaea would never subject you to Aquila's judgment.

**Dan Heng:** Hmm? You are...?

**Aglaea:** Hyacine. Thank you for taking care of the wounded over the past few days.

**Hyacine:** It's no trouble at all! The patients are very cooperative when we attend to them. I'm just thrilled to see them all going home healthy again. And ooh! It's Cassie! I've missed you!

> *(Trailblazer)* Who is this...?
> *(Trailblazer)* Grayie? Dannie?
> **Hyacine:** Aren't they just the cutest nicknames? Hehe.
> *(Trailblazer)* Cassie?
> **Castorice:** Um, Miss Hyacine, about that nickname... Could you—
> **Hyacine:** Aw, don't be shy. It's a cute nickname!
> *(Trailblazer)* Is Aglaea's nickname Aglie?
> **Aglaea:** Hehe...
> **Castorice:** Mis—!
> **Hyacine:** Wah! You can't say that! Lady Aglaea should always be "Lady Aglaea"!

**Dan Heng:** This girl... She's a natural at making friends.

> *(Trailblazer)* She reminds me of an old friend with pink hair.
> *(Trailblazer)* So am I!
> **Dan Heng:** I'll let you take it from here, then.

**Hyacine:** Hehe, as an acolyte of Aquila, I'm here to bring healing light to the world. Plus, I've heard you're also children of the distant skies! Fate must have brought us together! Can we be friends? I'd love to hear your tales of the sky! And ooh, it's a rare occasion that Okhema presents such an invaluable gift to guests. Lady Aglaea, may I have the honor of tasting it, too?

**Aglaea:** Of course you may. Esteemed guests of Okhema, please believe me when I say that this gift carries no hidden agendas. As for the matter of the alliance, you need not give me an answer here and now.

> *(Trailblazer)* Well, if you insist...
> *(Trailblazer)* Ohh, she's here to help Aglaea recruit us.
> **Hyacine:** Haha, you're mistaken! The capable Lady Aglaea doesn't need my help with things like this.

**Dan Heng:** ...I see. In that case, we will accept this gift from Okhema. We welcome the chance to talk with you, Lady Hyacine.

**Hyacine:** Yay! Then I'll get started on my questionnaire!

**Dan Heng:** Pardon the question, but I've been meaning to ask this for a while: micro-expressions, tone of voice, questionnaires... Are these all part of the usual skill set for Aquila's priests?

**Aglaea:** Allow me to do the honors. This young lady is Hyacine, assistant lecturer of the Nousporists at the Grove of Epiphany and head nurse at the medical institution known as the Twilight Courtyard. Many of the Chrysos Heirs suffered injuries during Nikador's attack on Okhema, so the Grove sent Lady Hyacine to treat the wounded in Okhema.

**Hyacine:** But what I'm really good at is helping people sort out what ails their mind and spirit. If you're ever feeling troubled, come find me! A little sunbathing in the courtyard will chase all your worries away!

**Dan Heng:** Ah, so you're a therapist. Thank you, we will keep that in mind.

---

**Hyacine:** Well, it looks like everything's sorted out now! The patients have also all recovered. I think it's time for me to head back, Lady Aglaea.

**Aglaea:** I would love to keep you in Okhema for a few more days, but I would hate to provoke the ire of the Grove. As it happens, Okhema intends to send a messenger to the Grove for updates about the research on the black tide. We also want to communicate our desire to discuss the retrieval of Cerces' Coreflame at the earliest convenience.

**Hyacine:** That... sounds pretty serious. Lady Aglaea, you can't be asking me to...

**Aglaea:** No need to fret. We won't put you on the spot. Trianne will be going there as the diplomatic messenger of Okhema, and Castorice will be accompanying her.

**Castorice:** I happen to have a few questions for Professor Anaxa as well, regarding the missing Titan Thanatos.

**Dan Heng:** Since this involves Okhema's diplomatic affairs, we'll give you some privacy.

> *(Trailblazer)* Let's go out and play!
> *(Trailblazer)* Back to bed again? Boring!

**Aglaea:** If you're not keen on just sitting around, (Trailblazer), why not go with them? The Grove is a picturesque place, perfect for sightseeing.

**Hyacine:** That's a wonderful suggestion! Come visit the Grove, Grayie! I'd be delighted to show you around!

**Dan Heng:** Sounds good. Gaining more insight into the customs and geography of different regions of Amphoreus is also part of Trailblazing.

**Aglaea:** In that case, do bring this spindle along with you. This tool, called the Weft, is used to spin thread and has been passed down in my family through generations. Though it doesn't see much practical use nowadays, it's become a symbol of great significance. In this respect, it is the perfect token to represent my will. If you encounter trouble at the Grove, just take this out and show it to them.

---

**(Trailblazer):** *(Castorice is right. I should get some rest. Mem is probably exhausted too. She hasn't made a peep.)*

**(Trailblazer):** *(I wonder how Dan Heng has been holding up these past few days.)*

*(Speak to Dan Heng)*
**Dan Heng:** Long time no see. I've heard about what happened from Phainon... You've been through a lot.

> *(Trailblazer)* Not at all. It's all in service to Trailblaze.
> **Dan Heng:** Heh... I wasn't expecting Amphoreus to temper the will of the Nameless.
> *(Trailblazer)* Life is a struggle.
> **Dan Heng:** ...May you never succumb to the dark side of Trailblaze.
> *(Trailblazer)* I missed you so much, my dearest friend!
> **Dan Heng:** ...It seems you took things pretty hard.

> *(Trailblazer)* What were you doing while I was gone?
> **Dan Heng:** I was organizing logs. I also asked Hyacine for some research material from the Grove.
> **Dan Heng:** I discovered something interesting. Do you remember that Amphoreus's sky was sealed away by a Titan? Aquila's presence keeps this world isolated from the rest of the world beyond the sky.
> **Dan Heng:** It seems Hyacine's ancestors were the Sky Priests who worshipped Aquila. Perhaps she can give us more clues to find a way back to the Astral Express.
> **Dan Heng:** Nothing out of the ordinary happened in the holy city in the past few days. I didn't have to talk to anyone either. It was kind of nice spending time alone.
> *(Trailblazer)* Just you wait. Your good times are coming to an end!
> **Dan Heng:** After what happened at the Grove, there's sure to be unrest in the coming days. We should rest for now.
> *(Trailblazer)* Awful! I should have stayed and organized documents with you.
> **Dan Heng:** ...I'm afraid you'd probably find the task of organizing just as awful.

**Dan Heng:** You look tired. You should get some sleep before we set off tomorrow...

**Dan Heng:** Oh, by the way, I heard some rumors... If you're going to wander around, I suggest avoiding the hot bath.

---

*(Switching to Tribbie's POV...)*

*Tribbie realized that Trianne, currently located at the Grove of Epiphany, had opened the Century Gate once again. Tribbie is now deeply anxious...*

### Part 91 — `databank/missions/chapter-02-light-slips.md`
**Context:** Mission 6: Passages, Knocking Echoes in Dreams > Look for Oronyx
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-02-light-slips__look-for-oronyx`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-02-light-slips.md`

**Phainon:** Everyone, we must take action now. We should split into two groups: One group will stay in the Abyss and meet up with Aglaea's reinforcements, then search for Trianne's whereabouts.

**Phainon:** The other group will come with me to pursue the black-robed swordmaster and recover the Coreflame.

**Dan Heng:** Do you know where it's headed?

**Phainon:** I didn't get to tell you this before, but Anaxa and I already have our misgivings about the black tide, so we've made preparations.

**Phainon:** I'll tell you the details as we move. Opportunities are fleeting on the battlefield. We should hurry.

**Okhema Scout:** Lord Phainon! Ah... Lady Tribbie! And Lady Trinnon! I'm glad you're alright!

**Phainon:** Did Aglaea's people get here?

**Okhema Scout:** Yes! Lady Aglaea already knows about the situation and sent along a message.

**Okhema Scout:** "Phainon, I understand that swordmaster is of great importance to you, but if you charge in without a plan, I fear that... you won't be the only one who dies in vain."

**Phainon:** ...I understand. "Hatred is a hero's deadliest poison." I have no intention of fighting alone. If that swordmaster really is related to the black tide, the Chrysos Heirs must not take them lightly.

**Phainon:** I have indeed planned to return to the holy city and discuss the strategy with Aglaea. We'll need all the help we can get for this encirclement.

**Dan Heng:** Why don't we split up as previously discussed? Hyacine and I will stay to regroup with the reinforcements. You go back to the holy city with everyone else and tell Aglaea about your plan.

**Trinnon:** Trinnon will stay in the Abyss, too. That way, both sides can communicate with each other if anything happens.

**Dan Heng:** ...Mydei?

**Mydei:** Outlanders... no, Trailblazers, I will soon make my way to Castrum Kremnos. If all goes according to plan, this may be our final meeting. I just want to let you know that I appreciate everything you have done for Amphoreus.

**Dan Heng:** Reflecting on the past and moving forward to the future — a challenge I've faced as well. Though we never had a chance for a deep conversation, after all we've experienced together... you are also an ally of the Trailblaze, Mydei.

**Mydei:** That means a lot to me. May success accompany your Trailblazing wherever it leads.

> *(Trailblazer)* I guess he will say something inspiring.
> *(Trailblazer)* I guess he will say something sentimental.
> *(Trailblazer)* I guess he will say something nonsensical.

**(Trailblazer):** *sob* ...What are we going to do without you?

**Mydei:** ...

**Dan Heng:** Just smile if you don't know how to respond.

**Mydei:** ...Haha.

**Mydei:** Now that I bear the divine within me, I can get the vague feeling that... that you have a great significance for the future of Amphoreus. With this departure, I fear that I will never again return. Can I entrust you both to watch my back... and to continue supporting the Flame-Chase Journey in my absence?

**Dan Heng:** ...Rest assured. We'll do everything we can.

**Mydei:** Hmph, well... It feels peculiar, I suppose? I'm not talking about your photographic skills. It's just that I rarely take photos... But this one... It's pretty good.

### Part 93 — `databank/missions/chapter-02-light-slips.md`
**Context:** Mission 9: Passage, Reveal the Past Once More > Dan Heng and the Trailblazer
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-02-light-slips__dan-heng-and-the-trailblazer`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-02-light-slips.md`

**Dan Heng:** I'm back. Finally have a moment to catch my breath. Want to chat, (Trailblazer)? It's unusual to see Mem isn't with you.

> *(Trailblazer)* Let Mem rest. They've had a tiring past few days.

**Dan Heng:** Since they're not here, let me ask you — **are you really sure you want to take on the trial of Oronyx's Coreflame on behalf of the Chrysos Heirs?**

> *(Trailblazer)* Absolutely! I don't see why not.
> *(Trailblazer)* Still on the fence. It was Mem's idea...
> *(Trailblazer)* It's about time for me to level up.

**Dan Heng:** ...We've ended up at this point after all. This is probably a fate that "Trailblaze" can't evade. You should be extra cautious and watch out for yourself. Based on the experiences of those before you, the trial is no walk in the park.

**Dan Heng:** But what concerns me even more is that you'll further bind yourself to Amphoreus... If you truly assume that Titan's divinity, do you believe you'll be able to lay it down again? Forget it. We'll deal with that when the time comes. No matter what happens, I'll be right there with you.

*(Dan Heng shares that messengers from the Council of Elders questioned him. Dark undercurrents are roiling in Okhema.)*

**Dan Heng:** The memory card is full. I bet March and the others are pretty worried too.

---

**Tribbie:** And so, this long day passed for Okhema. Some people arrived, and some left forever, just like every other day of this journey where we, stumbling, move forward. We have long been prepared. If we are to chase the flames, then we must grow used to farewells. And yet...

### Part 94 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Speak with Dan Heng — The Citizens' Assembly
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__speak-with-dan-heng-the-citizens-assembly`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** Citizens' assembly?

**Phainon:** That's right. Oh (Trailblazer), you're back. Just in time. This is something that the both of you should probably know.

> *(Trailblazer)* Some good news?
> **Phainon:** Good news? I wouldn't call it that.
> *(Trailblazer)* More trouble?
> **Phainon:** I wouldn't call it trouble. I just think it will be favorable if the both of you understand the situation in Okhema better.

**Dan Heng:** Do you remember the Council of Elders that was on our heels previously? It seems like they've made their move.

**Phainon:** Some background information for you: The Council of Elders is an authoritative body in Amphoreus made up of different city representatives. They hold legislative power over the holy city and are in charge of presiding over the citizens' assembly held once every few years. This is the most important social event of Okhema. It is highly influential... and can even affect what happens to the Flame-Chase Journey.

> *(Trailblazer)* So Aglaea doesn't actually call the shots?
> **Phainon:** Despite her vision and wisdom that spans across thousands of years, even she cannot win over the hearts of everyone.
> *(Trailblazer)* I have never once passed my sociology class.
> **Dan Heng:** ...Perhaps you should retake some exams next time we visit Paperfold University.

**Phainon:** Every five to ten years, the Council of Elders organizes a citizens' assembly at Dawncloud. During the assembly, the representatives will vote on major issues such as whether the Chrysos Heirs led by Aglaea should still hold the right to govern the holy city and... whether the Titan's conquest should continue.

**Dan Heng:** It sounds like some sort of system to balance the heroes and the mortals.

**Phainon:** That is correct. However, the problem lies in the fact that there are ill-intentioned extremists in the Council of Elders. They view the citizens' assembly as a way to restrain the Chrysos Heirs and hope to take complete control over the Flame-Chase Journey. We can refer to them as the opposition. During the assemblies held in the past hundred years, the voices supporting the opposition have been growing stronger in the shadows. It's only thanks to Aglaea that the Flame-Chase Journey has been able to continue to this day. The citizens' assembly is just around the corner, but this time, there seems to be a new variable to the situation...

> *(Trailblazer)* Is it us?
> **Dan Heng:** No. Although we might be mentioned, the Council of Elders has another target in mind.
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it Mem?
> **Dan Heng:** ...An interesting train of thought, but the Council of Elders has another target in mind.
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it Anaxa?
> **Dan Heng:** It looks like you've realized it as well.

**Phainon:** I'm afraid that the Council of Elders might want to take advantage of Professor Anaxa. Aglaea will surely take back Cerces' Coreflame. But with Anaxa's current condition... It's so unique that nobody knows how taking the Coreflame away from him will affect him. He has always been someone that is only concerned about his own research. I'm certain that he must want to escape from the monitoring of the golden thread and protect himself. If the opposition were to offer an olive branch at this time...

**Dan Heng:** You suspect that he might betray the Flame-Chase Journey and defect toward the Council of Elders?

**Phainon:** Anaxa has never believed in the prophecy, so technically it cannot be considered a betrayal. However, he has taught many Chrysos Heirs and has long been considered an important ally of Okhema. I truly do not wish to... see comrades turning on one another.

**Dan Heng:** I have a better understanding of the full picture now. Is there anything that we can do?

**Phainon:** As I believe in his character, I plan to plead with Aglaea and try to buy more time for him. Then, the greatest way in which you can help is for you to know what I am fighting for.

**Dan Heng:** We understand. Thank you for being honest with us. Things are piling up again... (Trailblazer) and Mem are tied up with Oronyx's trial, and I'm preparing to visit the Grove to uncover clues about the world beyond the sky.

> *(Trailblazer)* Let's just take things one step at a time. / *I just want to rest right now.*

**Phainon:** Allow me to apologize for the fact that we always seem to be troubling you. I hope that Oronyx will show more benevolence and be more accommodating. I remember that they seemed quite fond of you back in the Abyss. Go and rest and regain your strength. We'll meet at the Vortex in a few days.

---

### Part 97 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Wake Up in the Private Bath Chamber
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__wake-up-in-the-private-bath-chamber`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**(Trailblazer):** ...
**Dan Heng:** You're finally awake.

---

**Dan Heng:** I'll keep it simple — you succeeded. Oronyx has entrusted you with their divinity. Now, you're no different from a demigod of Amphoreus. However... ...Mem has told me about what happened during the trial. They are still explaining things to the others and told me to bring you back to your room to rest first.

> *(Trailblazer)* The Titan must be joking, right...? / *Don't sigh, Dan Heng...* / *So... what's my superpower?*

**Dan Heng:** ...For you to be able to say that even now... You're truly impressive. There's something that... I've never told you. When we landed on Amphoreus, our car was pierced by Nikador's lance. Do you remember? Back in the ruins of the Abyss, this was what I said: "You passed out when our car got hit. I blacked out too, but not before dragging you out."

**Dan Heng:** Actually... My account kind of made light of the situation. What actually happened was much more complicated. You were severely injured back then, with several wounds still bleeding profusely. I struggled to carry you out and used the last of my strength to stem the bleeding. But then, I was also hit by a falling rock and lost my consciousness. The last time I checked, you still weren't breathing. But when I regained consciousness... I saw that you were alive and full of energy, so I never brought this up again.

> *(Trailblazer)* How exactly did I come back to life?
> *(Trailblazer)* Haha, so you were knocked unconscious by a rock!
> **Dan Heng:** ...That's beside the point.

**Dan Heng:** I'm not sure whether the gaze of Fuli has anything to do with this matter, but according to the Titan, you are living in the form of a memory right now, just like those objects of the past in the miracles of Oronyx. Perhaps Mem is the anchor rope that ties you to the present. I had assumed they couldn't stray too far from you, but the truth... might be the exact opposite.

> *(Trailblazer)* What should we do next? / *Cry, cry for me.* / *Stellaron, do something!*
> **Dan Heng:** For a while, I also thought that the Stellaron was your life support system, but it seems like... this hypothesis has been disproven.

**Dan Heng:** It's not time for us to despair yet. There must be a way to save you. Oronyx mentioned "finding your stolen future" and "cheating death." Considering Mydei's indestructible body, it seems to suggest that there is some kind of miraculous power in Amphoreus that can even reverse death... The key seems to lie with the Hand of Shadow, whose whereabouts are unknown.

**Mem:** (Trailblazer)... Are you alright? I heard you two talking about Thanatos. Cas is also very concerned about the warning. She has left the Vortex and returned to the garden. Shall we go and look for her? Thanatos is the Titan that Cas has always been looking for. Regardless of whether there are any clues, there's probably no one else that understands Death better than her.

**Dan Heng:** Let's do that then, (Trailblazer).

---

**Kind:** collective-pointer

**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** ...
**Hyacine:** What are you thinking about?
**Dan Heng:** Just amazed at how this towering tree is Cerces' divine body. It's a majestic sight.
**Hyacine:** Is that so? You don't look the slightest bit amazed, though. Plus, you seem to be strangely familiar with the city around the Great Tree...?
**Dan Heng:** ...Merely something from the past that I won't go in to.
**Hyacine:** Events of the past, huh... I was also a child when I looked up at Cerces. Their leaves and branches reflected the sparkling stars, and their trunk was like a pillar that supported the sky and ground.
**Hyacine:** I stood at this very spot back then, imagining the day when I could climb to the top of this tree... Because I would be the closest I can be to the sky at that spot.
**Dan Heng:** You're a Sky Priest, yet you study at a place that is home to the Reason Titan... That's rather counterintuitive.
**Hyacine:** Don't read too much into it. I mean, can mortals even hope to reach Aquila's realm? They're a strict and draconian god, nothing like Cerces. Reason is willing to share what is theirs with others, but Sky... only expects people to revere them.
**Hyacine:** You get what I'm saying, right Dannie? After all, the Titan that imprisoned you and (Trailblazer) here in Amphoreus is none other than Aquila themself...
**Dan Heng:** Yes, I didn't count on provoking the ire of a local god.
**Hyacine:** But there will always be people who dream of soaring toward the sky. The Grove has a wealth of research on Aquila, and that is exactly what we are here for.
**Hyacine:** It's just... I never thought Grayie would meet with something like that before we set off... As a physician, I should have been there by her side.
**Dan Heng:** Bad luck tends to come in streaks, so don't beat yourself up over it. Besides, Aglaea needs you here to uncover the truth behind what happened to the Grove. Let's just make the most out of this trip and keep a lookout for any documents related to the Death Titan — they could be useful to (Trailblazer).
**Hyacine:** Yeah... There's no rest for the weary, huh.
**Hyacine:** In any case, what we're looking for could be anywhere in this enormous Grove. Let's split up to cover more ground. Dannie, with your skills, I'm sure those monsters born of the black tide will pose no threat to you anyway.
**Dan Heng:** What about you? Will you be alright on your own?
**Hyacine:** Hehe, have you forgotten that you're talking to the chief physician of the Twilight Courtyard? Have more faith in me. I'll be just fine!
**Dan Heng:** ...The silence in here is near deafening.
**Dan Heng:** With the vast amount of knowledge it holds, this academy is akin to a labyrinth of information... Even the cleverest of scholars would easily lose their way in here. I should gather my thoughts before heading in.

> *(Trailblazer)* How to leave Amphoreus...
> **Dan Heng:** (In the myths, Aquila, the Titan who reigns over the domain of the skies, is a colossal bird that overlooks the world beneath them with a hundred eyes. As long as they continue to soar above the clouds, the people of Amphoreus will remain trapped on the ground...)
> **Dan Heng:** (Hyacine's forebears were priests who worshiped Aquila. Nobody is more familiar with the taboos of the Sky Titan than her. Seeing how Aglaea has sent her here as a guide, it's possible that she plans to have Hyacine take on a trial in the future.)
> *(Trailblazer)* Amphoreus' Titan mythology...
> **Dan Heng:** (In Amphoreus, Titans are a common topic of discussion. People create myths that, in turn, conceive Titans... It's a tricky task to keep an objective view when among the people of Amphoreus.)
> **Dan Heng:** (Is there a way to step out of the situation and look at it from a different perspective?)
> *(Trailblazer)* Titans and Aeons...
> **Dan Heng:** (Titans and Aeons are two distinct classes of beings. Titans can communicate with us humans, and it is not impossible for us to overpower them. Upon their demise, we can even take over their divinity.)
> **Dan Heng:** (In this regard, Titans can be said to be a lot more similar to humans than Aeons are...)
> *(Trailblazer)* The Chrysos Heirs and demigods...
> **Dan Heng:** (Chrysos Heirs are humans who possess heroic qualities, with most of them demonstrating extraordinary combat prowess and strength. Upon clearing a Coreflame trial, a Chrysos Heir can even ascend to a demigod and inherit a Titan's divinity...)
> **Dan Heng:** (Wait, something about that feels off. Is this a... selection process of some sort?)
> *(Trailblazer)* The Flame-Chase Journey and the prophecy...
> **Dan Heng:** (Guided by the prophecy, the heroes are on a quest to reclaim Coreflames and usher in the Era Nova. Their efforts have seen gratifying results to date.)
> **Dan Heng:** (As I expected, the prophecy is the hardest to make any sense of. But with barely any information to go on, no matter how much I ruminate on it, it won't yield any results... I should leave this for another time.)
> *(Trailblazer)* Think of something else...
> **Dan Heng:** (I should probably turn my attention to something else.)
> *(Return to the previous options)*
> *(Trailblazer)* (Trailblazer)'s plight...
> **Dan Heng:** (Nobody could've ever imagined that (Trailblazer)'s life would be forfeited the moment we landed on Amphoreus...)
> **Dan Heng:** (I should have done more to keep her safe... but it's not too late. We still have a fighting chance — the unique rules that govern life and death on Amphoreus might just work in our favor and save her.)
> **Dan Heng:** (The Grove is a veritable library of knowledge... Maybe they'll have records on a similar case? Please let there be one...)
> *(Trailblazer)* Enough musing for now. It's time to move.
> **Dan Heng:** The clock is ticking... I should search for clues as I move.

### Part 100 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Investigate the Remains of the Black Tide Spawned Monster
**Kind:** personal
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__investigate-the-remains-of-the-black-tide-spawned-monster-2`

*(Before heading to the Library of Philia, look for any documents left behind along the way. Many documents were scattered when scholars fled from the black tide creatures. There might be information about Thanatos.)*

**Dan Heng:** The night sky feels unusual... now that I've gotten used to the eternal light of Okhema.

*(Approach the marked location)*
*(narrative)* Black monsters are twisted in form, and their life has turned to ash together with their souls. It's impossible to figure out their form when they were still alive.
**Dan Heng:** These are... the remains of a black tide monster. It does look different from the Titankin from up close.
**Dan Heng:** What's that underneath it...? A scroll?

---

*(Quick read)*
*(narrative)* **The Scholars' Debates (I)**
*(narrative)* *A recorded debate between Venerationist and Nousporist scholars. Most of it is illegible, leaving only fragments of dialogue...*

> **Venerationist Scholar:** ...We all recall that state that dared touch the sky. But what were they really? Just blind masses manipulated by ambitious politicians who dared challenge the Titans' authority.
> **Venerationist Scholar:** A completely meaningless attempt, an absolute mistake.
> **Nousporist Scholar:** Hmph, you Venerationists have bowed to the Titans for so long, you've forgotten a scholar's true purpose?
> **Nousporist Scholar:** "Making a mistake does not mean the act was meaningless."
> **Nousporist Scholar:** Even those students new to the Grove understand this from basic logic lessons.
> **Nousporist Scholar:** An entire city-state reduced to ashes proved the skyships' construction was a mistake. To me, that's precisely the meaning they achieved through sacrifice.
> **Venerationist Scholar:** Am I hearing this right? Such a statement... are you converting to Venerationism?
> **Nousporist Scholar:** No, I'm simply examining the matter from another perspective.
> **Venerationist Scholar:** ...
> *(narrative)* *...Legible content ends here.*

---

**Dan Heng:** As I expected, the Grove doesn't forbid discussions about the Sky. It's a debate between two scholars about the skyships... Aglaea also made mention of the ships when she interrogated us — a memory I'd rather forget.
**Dan Heng:** I'll hold onto this for now. With any luck, this journey won't leave us empty-handed.

---

---

### Part 101 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Continue Forward and Join Up with Hyacine
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__continue-forward-and-join-up-with-hyacine`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

*(Before heading to the Library of Philia, look for any documents left behind along the way. Let's exchange information with Hyacine.)*

**Dan Heng:** ...Huh?

*(Approach the marked location)*
**Dan Heng:** That's... Hyacine? Why is she surrounded by so many monsters? ...As much faith as I have in her, it's probably a good idea to help her out there.
**Hyacine:** Really now? This is way more trouble than I could've asked for.
**Dan Heng:** Need a hand?
**Hyacine:** Hm... Or you could sit back and watch?
**Dan Heng:** I can't tell if you're serious or not... But being hard to read isn't without its perks, I suppose.
**Hyacine:** Hehe, I'm kidding, Dannie! Lend me a hand, pretty please? Alright, now let's get them!

*(Begin battle against Black Tide's Corroded Axe ×2, Tide-Eroded Blade ×2)*

---

*(After the battle)*
**Dan Heng:** This place is practically in ruins. What happened? There shouldn't be this many monsters here.
**Hyacine:** The black tide is remarkably quick to detect the light of Amphoreus, so I... or rather, the blessings of the Sky Priest in me, might have unknowingly called them to me.

---

### Part 102 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Speak with Hyacine
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__speak-with-hyacine`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** Anaxa... goras. He's the scholar who fused himself with the Coreflame of Reason.
**Hyacine:** This is new. I didn't know Professor Anaxa was researching the Sky. He's never mentioned anything about it.
**Dan Heng:** Phainon said Anaxa might be opposed to the Flame-Chase Journey.
**Hyacine:** "Might" is an understatement. Anaxa has never been one to venerate the gods, nor has he ever found it within himself to put any faith in the prophecy. People have come to think of him as a heretic, even in a place as liberal as the Grove.
**Hyacine:** Yet, it is also this very "blasphemer" that Cerces favors. Fate sure works in mysterious ways.
**Dan Heng:** I picked up a scroll on my way here, too. It looks like it may be related to the one you're holding.
**Hyacine:** Oh? Let me have a look.

*(Hyacine combines both records together...)*
**Hyacine:** By the looks of them, these scrolls are records of two conversations that took place in the same setting, one after the other.
**Dan Heng:** There's still much I don't know about the Grove. Do you mind if I ask you a few questions?
**Hyacine:** Sure, go ahead.

> *(Trailblazer)* Who are the Venerationists?
> **Hyacine:** The Grove of Epiphany is home to seven schools of thought. While all seven schools are founded on the ideologies of Thalesus the First Scholar, each is unique in its own way. Among them, the Venerationists revere and worship the Titans the most.
> **Hyacine:** As the sage of the Venerationists, Euthyphro has always been at loggerheads with Anaxa.
> **Hyacine:** If we were to place the Seven Sages on the scales of Talanton, Anaxa and Euthyphro would be placed on opposite sides to achieve balance.
> **Dan Heng:** A very illustrative explanation. Thank you.
> *(Trailblazer)* Was it a mistake to build the skyships?
> **Hyacine:** Nearly everyone in Amphoreus has heard the story of the skyships by now. Aquila's stature is loftier than the heavens, and their divine retribution is merciless.
> **Hyacine:** That's why it's no use building giant sky-faring ships... Oh, but I do wonder if a supercharged mini Tribbie cannon could slip past their watch?
> **Dan Heng:** That was a joke... right?
> *(Trailblazer)* What is Anaxa's field of study?
> **Hyacine:** Nousporists primarily study the application of alchemy in soul repair.
> **Hyacine:** Everyone considers Anaxa a blasphemer, but that has never stopped him from sharing his ideas, no matter how taboo. If you must say that he's hiding something... then it's likely what lies beneath his eye patch.
> *(Trailblazer)* I don't have any more questions.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa often says that... "Every failure brings us one step closer to the truth."
> **Hyacine:** Perhaps, to him, the crash of the skyship carries a different interpretation. The firmament is not impenetrable. On the contrary, it was precisely because the skyship represented the first step toward breaking free from the shackles of the earth that it provoked the wrath of the Sky Titan and brought divine punishment upon itself.
> **Hyacine:** It's a shame that the majority of the Grove's research on Aquila was headed by the Venerationists, with sacrificial rituals making up a predominant part of their findings. Discourse about more esoteric subject matters in relation to Aquila dwindled even further after the skyship incident, so much so that even this scroll is completely new to me.
> **Dan Heng:** Don't worry about it. We'll just collect everything we can find on the Titans for now.
> **Dan Heng:** There's something else that's caught my attention, though... This says that Anaxa is conducting secret experiments of some sort?
> **Hyacine:** That's what I'd like to know. What are these experiments he's involved in? Even I, the assistant lecturer, know next to nothing about them...

---

### Part 103 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Head to the Library of Philia
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__head-to-the-library-of-philia`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Hyacine:** Other than books written by the seven schools, you can also find philosophical texts, ballads, and religious writings from the outlands here. Cities that desired to learn from the Grove were apparently expected to contribute knowledge of equal value to what they sought.
**Hyacine:** We've had more than our fair share of hilarious episodes over the years. For instance, it's said that the Erythrokeramists once borrowed a manuscript from a certain author but ended up returning a copy instead of the original! What's more, the original manuscript was supposedly then hidden here.
**Dan Heng:** That... doesn't seem like a nice thing to do.
**Hyacine:** Nice? No need to hold yourself back, Dannie. You can just tell it like it is: Their behavior was no different from bandits!
**Dan Heng:** Well, if you don't mind: Did the Grove ever rob Castrum Kremnos?
**Hyacine:** You've got me there. Some said you couldn't even find a dictionary in Castrum Kremnos, so I guess they were spared.
**Hyacine:** There are too many books in the Library of Philia. Flipping through them all will take too long. Why don't we use the biblioslate to search for the texts we need? We can bring them back to Okhema for reading after that.
**Dan Heng:** Agreed, this place isn't safe. We should leave as soon as we're done.
**Dan Heng:** There are still several more scrolls strewn all over the place. I'll gather them while you pull up those texts, this way, nothing will be overlooked.

---

---

### Part 104 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Search for the Materials Left by Anaxa
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__search-for-the-materials-left-by-anaxa`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

*(Head to the Library of Philia with Hyacine to collect materials for the data bank and expand our understanding of Amphoreus. Look for documents related to Anaxagoras.)*

*(Investigate the scroll to the left of the stone statue, optional)*
> *(Trailblazer)* Investigate
> *(narrative)* A book covered in annotations from many scholars. Its title is *The Art of Sophistry: 40 Ways to Improve Your Debating Skills*.
> *(narrative)* You skim through it, and the highlighted methods include virtual causation, false premises, contradiction conversion, conflating prevarication and reality, misinterpreted punctuation, misattribution, and generalizing specificity, among others.
> *(narrative)* Amidst it are scholars' exclamations: "A true eye-opener, the art of language knows no bounds," "If I can't master sophistry, I can at least learn to defend myself against them as an honest man," and so on.

**Dan Heng:** The last resort is... "When all else fails, employ brute force."
**Dan Heng:** ...This isn't going to be of any help, that's for sure.

*(Investigate the scroll to the right of the stone statue, optional)*
> *(Trailblazer)* Investigate
> *(narrative)* A record of the many research topics a Caprist scholar had applied for, and the replies from their school's Sage about these topics...
> *(narrative)* Topic 1: A discovery that many mythical beasts renowned for their longevity actually reside in areas with poorly managed birth and death records for such creatures. Permission to research. Reply: Lacks research significance, not approved.
> *(narrative)* Topic 2: An observation of real plants within the Grove imitating the growth patterns of nearby White Dryads. Permission to research. Reply: Falls under the domain of the Lotophagist school, not approved.
> *(narrative)* Topic 3: Investigating the feasibility of placing flying birds into rocket tubes to guide the direction of the rocket's flight. Permission to research. Reply: Too fanciful, not approved.
> *(narrative)* ...The subsequent topics grew increasingly eclectic, yet the outcome of the replies remained unchanged.

**Dan Heng:** Impressive tenacity... Hopefully it helped him survive the black tide.

*(Investigate the scroll in front of the stone statue)*
> *(Trailblazer)* Investigate
> *(narrative)* These are complaint letters from the scholars about Anaxagoras "The Blasphemer." There's a thick stack of letters.
> *(narrative)* Based on the dust accumulated and the perfectly intact seals, the complainee did not even bother opening them and just chucked them aside into a corner.

**Dan Heng:** This is... about Anaxa? I should read this. Hyacine, you should come have a look, too.

---

**Hyacine:** Ack, it's so dusty in here... Whoa, are these all complaints about him?
**Hyacine:** I can already see what they must have written: "Anaxa has no respect for the gods! His very existence undermines order in our society!" ...They're probably all things like that.
**Dan Heng:** Does Anaxa hold that much influence?
**Hyacine:** Why else would they call him "the Great Performer"? When Anaxa established his school of thought, the first thing he apparently did was read his own work to everyone.
**Hyacine:** "Are the Titans truly an accurate reflection of what humans think gods should be? Worlds created by gods are indubitably perfect, but what of Amphoreus? Only foolishness and insensitivity everywhere you turn!"
**Dan Heng:** ...An uncanny impersonation. What happened after?
**Hyacine:** They marched him straight to the seat of judgment before he could finish.
**Hyacine:** Though what he took on in the end was more a debate than an actual trial. He had to address every one of the scholars' allegations, or face expulsion from the Grove.
**Hyacine:** The first allegation was... ahem. "Do you agree that since the Titans created the heavens and earth, so the mountains, rivers, and seas must likewise be their creations?"
**Dan Heng:** ...Am I supposed to put myself in Anaxa's shoes and answer that?

> *(Trailblazer)* Yes.
> **Dan Heng:** As told in the myths, the seas, the skies, and the earth are all, beyond the shadow of a doubt, creations of the Titans.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa would've probably been spared a lot of trouble if he had answered this way... Instead, he quoted past instances of the Flame-Chase Journey and posited that humans have the potential to take over the divinity of Titans.
> *(Trailblazer)* No.
> **Dan Heng:** Based on the history of the Flame-Chase Journey, humans have the potential to take over the divinity of Titans.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa said the same thing then.
> **Hyacine:** Needless to say, his critics didn't take kindly to his answer, so they followed up with a second allegation.
> **Hyacine:** "You claim to be studying souls, yet you are, in fact, defiling them and committing acts condemned by the gods. Do you admit it?"
> **Dan Heng:** That's a serious allegation. Did he really do that?
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa always wears an eye patch, never removing it, even when alone. Some say he used one of his eyes as an alchemical ingredient.
> **Hyacine:** Every being in the world owes their safekeeping to Kephale, and this is even more so for the Chrysos Heirs who have divine blood coursing through them. If he has really done something of that nature, then it'd indeed be sacrilegious.
> **Hyacine:** Thankfully, they had no way to prove it, so it amounted to nothing more than an allegation in the end.
> **Hyacine:** One by one, Anaxa engaged in wars of words against those who would challenge him. Toward the end, a scholar asked him, "Anaxagoras, have you any respect for the gods?"
> **Hyacine:** It was only after a brief silence that he answered, "I have never denied that Titans are meant to be noble constructs."
> **Dan Heng:** What he didn't say — that the Titans are not noble — rings louder in this case.
> **Hyacine:** Yes... I'm not sure how that controversy was eventually resolved, but what's for certain at least is that Anaxa was allowed to stay in the Grove where he continued his research, despite being regarded as a heretic.
> **Hyacine:** By the way, this stack of complaint letters has reminded me of a place in the Grove... We may find clues there that aren't on the teleslate.
> **Hyacine:** I'm talking about Anaxa's alchemy lab, hidden between the cornerstones.
> **Dan Heng:** Given the Grove's meticulous approach to archiving, could there really be such an oversight right under their noses?
> **Hyacine:** It's a well-kept secret. Among the Seven Sages, excluding the nominal Chief Sage — each has a private sanctuary where they can conduct research undisturbed in their free time.

---

### Part 105 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Search for Anaxa's Lab Between the Cornerstones
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__search-for-anaxa-s-lab-between-the-cornerstones`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Dan Heng:** These trees... They're like a maze.

*(Approach the marked location)*
**Hyacine:** This is the place, but... where is everything?
**Hyacine:** Materials for alchemical experiments, matrix diagrams, and even the seal to the lab... They're all gone. Did the black tide destroy it all?
**Dan Heng:** No wonder we didn't see any defense mechanisms along the way.
**Dan Heng:** Have you ever been here before?
**Hyacine:** No, not even close. Not only are alchemy experiments high-risk activities, but Professor Anaxa also has a penchant for using bizarre materials. Students often saw him walk in looking right as rain but come out covered in soot with his hair singed. Naturally, no one dared to come near this place.

---

### Part 106 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Context:** Chapter 3 — Through the Petals in the Land of Repose (v3.2) > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Search for the Materials Left by Anaxa
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-03-through-petals__search-for-the-materials-left-by-anaxa-2`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-03-through-petals.md`
**Witness:** spoke

*(Investigate the scroll on top of the broken shelf)*
> *(Trailblazer)* Investigate
> *(narrative)* These are the materials requested by Anaxagoras. Documents from various fields have been compiled in one place, including "Titan Creations," "The Universality of the Soul," "Alchemy Doctrine," and so on.
> *(narrative)* Most of the materials look to be within the research domain of the Nousporist school of thought. However, a document titled *A Study on the Chrysos Heirs' Background* is mixed within.

---

*(Quick read)*
*(narrative)* **A Hastily Written Journal (I)**
*(narrative)* *These appear to be quick notes, examining the background of Chrysos Heirs...*

> *(narrative)* The Holy Maiden of Janusopolis — Tribbie, Trianne, and Trinnon, descendants of the Holy Maiden from the Temple of the Three Fates...
> *(narrative)* Goldweaver Aglaea of Okhema... from the Mnestia priestly family...
> *(narrative)* The Prince of Castrum Kremnos... Hyacine of the Twilight Courtyard... All of their ancestry have varying degrees of connection to the divine Titans or their divine authorities.
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Does the golden blood... come from the heritage of priest families... or royal bloodlines...?
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Anaxagoras — I myself show no such connection.
> *(narrative)* Kephale the Creator... what criteria did they use to create the golden blood... to shape the Chrysos Heirs?
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Yet Castorice, the Servant of Death from Aidonia... her lineage is neither Titan priest nor demigod... but she has walked the earth for at least a millennium with the power to grant "Death" to humans... Where did this power come from?
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Another text speaks of the dragon legends of Styxia... The alchemical extraction of "soul" ripped from dragon corpses is intriguing...
> *(narrative)* Dragons appear to be the Titans' creations...
> *(narrative)* Perhaps there's a way to reverse the process — using alchemy to re-fuse human "souls" with Titan creations, increasing the purity and divinity of golden blood toward the absolute?
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* This theory must be tested. Experiments to be arranged.

---

**Hyacine:** What's going on...? Anaxa is conducting research on our friends in Okhema and... me?
**Dan Heng:** This journal contains several intriguing points...

> *(Trailblazer)* Creations of Titans...
> **Dan Heng:** From the Abyss of Fate to Castrum Kremnos, I've seen many a Titan creation during my time in Amphoreus. Lifeforms created by Cerces can also be found in the Grove.
> **Dan Heng:** But fusing them with human souls through alchemical means? I can't even begin to imagine...
> *(Trailblazer)* The Chrysos Heirs...
> **Dan Heng:** Anaxa seems to hold special interest in the divinity coursing through the Chrysos Heirs' veins, and has even conducted a thorough investigation into the heroes' backgrounds.
> **Hyacine:** It wouldn't have been so strange if he had focused only on Cassie, given her own curiosity about her origins. Plus, considering that the Nousporists have crafted omnipotent medicines capable of curing diseases, it also makes sense that he would conduct research on immortal Chrysos Heirs.
> **Hyacine:** But now, it seems Anaxa's objective was never to create medicine but to identify the origin of the golden blood and figure out its inner workings.
> *(Trailblazer)* Anaxa's research...
> **Dan Heng:** Through alchemy, Anaxa aims to refine the golden blood in the Chrysos Heirs to a degree of purity equal to "divinity."
> **Dan Heng:** This must be what he means by "close to true purity." But... treating both Titan creations and the human body as materials to be used in alchemy experiments? That's rather extreme.
> **Hyacine:** ...
> **Hyacine:** Dannie, look at this. I have a bad feeling about this...

---

*(Quick read)*
*(narrative)* **A Hastily Written Journal (II)**
*(narrative)* *These documents appear incomplete. Judging from what's left behind, the important alchemical formulae have been taken away — only the author's reasoning remains...*

> *(narrative)* The previous approach... has flaws.
> *(narrative)* Fusing human "souls" with Titan creations does not purify golden blood.
> *(narrative)* Even pure Titan creations are contaminants to the Chrysos Heirs' golden blood.
> *(narrative)* Soul fusion between humans and creations merely repairs impurities of the soul or maintains a connection to dying souls.
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* While creation myths tell us that Chrysos Heirs and other creations were all made by Titans, their nature differs fundamentally.
> *(narrative)* The Titans' creations are no intermediate products of an alchemical process, and the Chrysos Heirs are closer to the Titans than the creations.
> *(narrative)* In other words, I should not have considered Titan creations equal to Chrysos Heirs.
> *(narrative)* The hypothesis I should focus on is one pointed towards the Titans themselves.
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Such rare chances to study Titans demand a meticulous selection of research subjects.
> *(narrative)* Living Titans prove impossible to restrain.
> *(narrative)* And dead Titans offer no insights worth pursuing.
> *(narrative)* But dying Titans — they are the perfect research subjects.
> *(narrative)* ...
> *(narrative)* Only by understanding the fundamentals of creation can we ensure perfection at the moment of everything's birth.
> *(narrative)* Through my own hands, I will melt a Titan and reach the true essence of the soul...

---

**Hyacine:** My biggest fear... It's come true... After going through his notes, I think I can piece it all together now:
**Hyacine:** Anaxa was carrying out experiments on soul transmutation.
**Dan Heng:** Do you mean he's trying to reconstruct human souls alchemically?
**Hyacine:** Yes, this is what he was secretly conducting research on — something he had to keep hidden from others. Compared to his public speeches, this is the real "blasphemy"...
**Hyacine:** Ah! I just thought of something...
**Dan Heng:** What's wrong?
**Hyacine:** Do you remember? When the black tide was approaching, the Grove had planned to send Cerces' Coreflame to a safe place, but an accident happened along the way, and Anaxa lost his life in the process...
**Dan Heng:** But Cerces took over his body and "resurrected" him, which led to the situation we're in now.
**Hyacine:** That's right... but what if Professor Anaxa's fusion with the Coreflame of Reason wasn't an accident?
**Dan Heng:** What do you mean?
**Hyacine:** Put yourself in his shoes. Knowing that the black tide was closing in and your time was running out, what would you have done?
**Dan Heng:** ...
**Dan Heng:** ...I'd put my theories to the test, no matter the cost.
**Hyacine:** Exactly. **When the black tide came, the true reason Anaxa headed to the Luminary Throne was... to transmute and fuse himself with the Titan's Coreflame.**
**Dan Heng:** Did he succeed?
**Hyacine:** No. Judging from how he reacted after the event, the results came as a surprise. Maybe he failed to consider the possibility that Cerces would make a similar decision.
**Hyacine:** The manuscripts detailing all the alchemical formulae have been taken away. His research... is still ongoing.
**Dan Heng:** Could he have taken them for other purposes?
**Hyacine:** Like what? There's only one Titan left in the world now—
**Hyacine:** ...
**Hyacine:** No... no way...
**Dan Heng:** "To conceive an entity of perfection, one must first understand the principles of creation"... If Anaxa is in Okhema now, then that means...
*(Screen fades to black)*
**Hyacine & Dan Heng:** ...He's after Kephale!

---

---

### Part 110 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 2 — Scrolls, Turn the Blade's Gaze > Head to the "Abyss of Fate" Janusopolis — Dan Heng & Lygus
**Kind:** personal
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__head-to-the-abyss-of-fate-janusopolis-dan-heng-lygus`

> *A few days later, at the crash site in the Abyss of Fate...*

**Lygus:** ...So, this is the car of the Astral Express you've spoken of. My repository of memories has been enriched with another fascinating piece of information. Thank you for leading me here, Lord Dan Heng.

**Dan Heng:** I'm surprised a Council Elder like yourself would be willing to journey so far to inspect the Express.

**Lygus:** Okhemans hold wisdom and knowledge in high esteem. If the citizens knew of your origins, I'm sure more would be intrigued by "the Trailblaze." Sadly, discussions about the "world beyond the sky" remain taboo.

**Lygus:** It's been quite some time since you first arrived in Okhema. Presumably you've been looking for a way to leave ever since?

**Dan Heng:** I've been actively gathering clues and testing different things. Sadly, the more I find out, the more powerless I feel.

**Lygus:** Don't be too hard on yourself, my lord. This puzzle has stumped Amphoreus' scholars for centuries. It isn't something you can solve overnight... even someone as gifted as yourself.

**Dan Heng:** In the Grove, I discovered extensive research on the Sky Titan. It appears that many scholars from Amphoreus believe the taboo against the "world beyond the sky" stems from a curse cast by Aquila.

**Lygus:** Exactly. Of the Twelve Titans of Amphoreus, Aquila is known for being the most capricious. The weather itself reflects their mood: Tranquil sunshine, mournful rain. Aquila's breath is a brisk wind, and their cries resound as thunder in a storm.

**Dan Heng:** Additionally, Aquila is one of the two Titans whose Coreflames have yet to be returned.

**Lygus:** The sky above affects every citizen. It's clear that Lady Aglaea's decision to confront Aquila at the end of the Flame-Chase Journey was a carefully considered strategy.

**Dan Heng:** If returning Aquila's Coreflame can break the sky's curse, we won't have to exhaust ourselves searching for a way back. We can simply focus on aiding the Chrysos Heirs.

**Lygus:** Antikytherans have no home to return to, so I understand your concern for your old friends and homeland. I used Antikytheran technology — or "scanning," as you might call it — to assess this car. Assuming my judgment is correct, I might be able to conduct a thorough overhaul of its "power systems."

**Dan Heng:** Really? But...

**Lygus:** Of course, that's only if you, my lord, permit me to interact with it.

**Dan Heng:** ...By all means, please proceed. But be careful, the Express's thrusters are extremely delicate.

**Lygus:** You honor me with your trust. I will be very careful.

> *Lygus carefully scans the damaged propulsion system, then methodically began inspecting its components...*

**Dan Heng:** This... is unbelievable. You seem to understand its structure perfectly. Have you repaired something like this before?

**Lygus:** Not exactly. But we Antikytherans are inherently sensitive to machinery. Kephale endowed our kind with a unique advantage for analyzing and learning. If you give me some time, I might be able to fully restore this damaged car to its former glory. Then you and your esteemed companion could set off on your return journey sooner.

**Dan Heng:** But... isn't the real issue with Aquila?

**Lygus:** Hypothetically, Lord Dan Heng, if I could open up a path out of Amphoreus that circumvents Aquila's divine punishment... Would you consider ceasing your support for the Flame-Chase Journey?

**Dan Heng:** I don't understand... Does the Council of Elders possess some secret means of counteracting divine punishment?

**Lygus:** It's best if you don't delve too deeply into that question, my lord. All you need to know is, I can make it happen.

**Dan Heng:** (Maybe this is not a decision by the Council of Elders but rather his personal wish...)

**Lygus:** The crucial point is, if you and your companion are unwilling to halt your support for the Chrysos Heirs... it could offend certain interests.

**Dan Heng:** Hmm... Forgive me, Lord Lygus, but I must be frank. When visiting Dawncloud, I reviewed the records of many Council Elders... Among them, only your background was nearly blank. I'd like to accept your goodwill, but given such a mysterious background and motives, anyone cautious would have doubts.

**Lygus:** Your prudence impresses me, just as expected...

**Dan Heng:** Are these people— Is this a trap? Is that why you accompanied me here?

**Lygus:** Please don't misunderstand, Lord Dan Heng. As the Theoros, I have no allegiance to their plans nor am I obliged to obstruct them. I am neutral. Out of respect for you, I hope to offer a peaceful solution to ensure the safety of you and your companion.

**Dan Heng:** ...One against four, then. Just to confirm: if things turn ugly later... will you help?

**Lygus:** I will not. As I said, I remain neutral. Are you asking this because you plan to refuse my offer and continue supporting the Chrysos Heirs?

**Dan Heng:** You weren't wrong about one thing, Lord Lygus. Indeed, we must try to return to our companions... But our "companions" now also include the Flame-Chasing Chrysos Heirs. The Nameless cannot and will not ignore the fate of our companions.

**Lygus:** So I see. This is what you call "Trailblazing."

**Dan Heng:** When I asked if you'd help, I meant... the ones who need help are them.

---

---

### Part 112 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 4 — Golden Thread, Relay the Savior's Fate > Go to the Marmoreal Palace
**Kind:** personal
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__go-to-the-marmoreal-palace`

**Dan Heng:** These folks in black... Are not responding to provocations, nor do they seem intent on fighting. What exactly are they waiting for?

**Lygus:** Pressure. Fear. Planting seeds of anxiety. It seems this is the strategy they've chosen.

**Dan Heng:** An organization that sees it as their mission to eradicate the Chrysos Heirs... I've read some chilling accounts — stories of the "Cleaners" using alchemical arts to implant fragments of memories into their successors, ensuring both identity and hatred live on.

**Lygus:** From what I know, these stories are true. The Caenis we know today — strictly speaking — is the 27th Caenis. Objectively, the rise of the Cleaners was both inevitable and necessary. Yet, their ruthless and twisted methods earned them nothing but disdain and condemnation from history.

**Dan Heng:** And still, they endure. An organization persisting through time, now working tirelessly to obstruct the Flame-Chase.

**Lygus:** Even in these crumbling end times, there are always those who find ways to profit from the chaos. To ask the vested interests to abandon their status, wealth, and ambitions in this world for a distant ideal, may be no easier than creating a miracle.

**Dan Heng:** ...I wonder where (Trailblazer) is now. If they're targeting me, they definitely won't escape their notice either. I hope all is well... Is that... Lord Krateros?

**Krateros:** There you are, Lord Dan Heng. We couldn't reach you on the teleslate, so the Holy City Guard almost assumed you and (Trailblazer) had met some terrible fate. You pests crawling in the shadows, back off! Your plot ends here. You've played your hand. Run while you still can. It might be your only chance to survive. Stay away from Okhema forever. If you set foot there again, the fury of its people will tear you apart. **Because Aglaea is dead, and in the eyes of the holy city's citizens, her blood is on your hands!**

---

---

### Part 113 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 4 — Golden Thread, Relay the Savior's Fate > Meet Up with Dan Heng — The Baths
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__meet-up-with-dan-heng-the-baths`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Phainon:** If this is all part of a larger plan, Tribbie must know something. She just couldn't say it in the message. Let's go find her.

*(The baths are empty. No citizen feels like bathing after hearing such terrible news.)*

**Phainon:** Dan Heng came back before us. Maybe he's heard something...

**Dan Heng:** Ah, you're back. How's everything? No injuries, I hope?

**Phainon:** Don't worry. The Cleaners didn't lay a hand on us. Lord Krateros, were you in the city when it happened? What exactly went down...

**Krateros:** Regrettably, no one knows the details of that moment. ...Eyewitnesses at the Marmoreal Palace saw Aglaea fall from the Chrysos Heir Bath. She showed no signs of life by the time the Holy City Guards approached to check. They were too afraid to jump to conclusions... After all, she was a demigod who'd lived for over a thousand years, the leader of the Chrysos Heirs. Most citizens of this city believed that death couldn't touch her. She was pierced through the heart by a uniquely crafted dagger. I saw it for myself. It's one of the weapons carried by the "Cleaners."

> *(Trailblazer)* Why didn't they go after us?
> **Phainon:** I also don't understand... This doesn't add up.
> *(Trailblazer)* She really was assassinated...
> **Phainon:** No, that doesn't make sense... The ones who appeared in the Grove, and those who confronted Dan Heng in the Abyss, didn't immediately resort to violence. They were just watching and trying to intimidate. They didn't have any reason, nor the courage, to attack Aglaea.

**Dan Heng:** Speculating blindly won't help. Let's go find Lady Tribbie, as she understands Aglaea's mind best.

**Krateros:** I'll stay here to lead the guards and maintain order. Find out what happened — but don't take too long. With Aglaea gone, someone has to take her place and lead Okhema. Also, Miss Hyacine was the one who declared Aglaea dead. You might want to speak with her.

---

### Part 114 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`

**Dan Heng:** We heard from Krateros. Are you... alright?

**Hyacine:** I'm fine... I've been staying close to Lady Tribbie and Lady Trinnon after the incident. It's great that you could come back so quickly... They need someone with them now.

### Part 117 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 5 — Grove, Judge the Past and Present > Third Ancestor — Grandmother Jacyntha (at Castrum Kremnos)
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__third-ancestor-grandmother-jacyntha-at-castrum-kremnos`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Hyacine:** We're here. This is it.

**Dan Heng:** Castrum Kremnos...

**Hyacine:** Lord Mydei has been fighting the black tide here alone day after day... I'm worried about him.

**Dan Heng:** He sent a message earlier, carried by thunder, warning us to watch out for the Flame Reaver's actions.

**Hyacine:** There — that sleeping spirit... is my grandmother.

**Dan Heng:** Your... grandmother?

**Hyacine:** Yes. My grandmother Jacyntha was a warrior. She represented the Twilight Courtyard in the Kremnos Festival. She fought her way through every challenge and reached the final stage. She was just one step away from securing a peace treaty for the Skyfolk, but... was ultimately defeated by the champion warrior of that time. But behind the glorious duel... was a tragic end. Not long after the festival, my grandmother's injuries worsened, and she passed away far from home.

**Dan Heng:** And the Kremnoan warrior she fought against...

**Hyacine:** ...was Lord Krateros.

**Dan Heng:** ... Yet you still saved his life, even though he took your family.

**Hyacine:** Seeing him lying there, gravely wounded, bleeding out... I can't put what I felt at that moment into words. But when I heard Lord Krateros took that fatal blow to protect Lady Trianne... All those tangled feelings faded away. To quote Lord Phainon, Krateros must have done that "to become a better man."

**Dan Heng:** It takes true courage to forgive. You're every bit as strong as your ancestors, Hyacine.

**Hyacine:** Heh... You're getting better at this, Dannie. In her final diary entry, my grandmother wrote that when the moment of destiny comes, a descendant of the Sky people would deliver the message of their departure to her spirit. I wonder what she'd think... When the one awaking her turns out to be her beloved granddaughter.

---

### Part 120 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 9 — Dawn, Shine at the World's End > Hyacine's Ascension
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__hyacine-s-ascension`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Witness:** spoke

*(Dan Heng opens the spirit water path. Hyacine bids farewell.)*

**Dan Heng:** **"At the end of the rainbow bridge, the Skyfolk will mend dusk and dawn."** Lady Tribios's prophecy... It seems it didn't fail after all.

**Hyacine:** It still feels unreal, even after seeing it a second time... You'd move in perfect harmony with the rivers and seas of this world.

**Dan Heng:** But we can't stay. (Trailblazer) and I... we have people waiting for us, a home calling us back.

**Hyacine:** The Astral Express, isn't it? March 7th, the one you always mention... I'd like to meet her someday. Of course, I do feel sadness. A sharp, aching sadness... But as a healer from the Twilight Courtyard, it's my duty to stay composed. A sorrowful expression would only add to their burden. The world I love is the one suffering now. How could I face it with a frown?

---

### Part 121 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 9 — Dawn, Shine at the World's End > Return to Okhema — Darkness Falls
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__return-to-okhema-darkness-falls`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Witness:** spoke

**Phainon:** Okhema's Dawn has come to an end... Why is this happening...?

**Dan Heng:** The sky is burning.

*(They descend the mountain, fighting black tide creatures. They reach Dawncloud, where Caenis prays madly before Kephale's empty Coreflame casket.)*

**Caenis:** O Sky Father Kephale, please forgive these ignorant and lowly children of humanity... They are so utterly naive that they think of the golden-blooded heretics as Deliverers... This bloodred sky, and the raging flames and demons throughout the land, will purge their foolishness...

**Phainon:** Caenis...

**Caenis:** Ah... Lord "Deliverer"? Why don't you join me in praying to the benevolent Kephale...? Cast your eyes on this ravaged land... All of this, sins committed by you blasphemous heretics...

> *(Trailblazer)* She... looks like she's got a screw loose.
> **Dan Heng:** Mm... We should not waste our time on a pitiful madwoman.

**Phainon:** Dan Heng is right. My sword yearns to end your pathetic life... But to a cornered dog like you, the most deserving punishment is to personally witness the Chrysos Heirs fulfill their mission. Only then... will you truly know how meaningless and foolish your past transgressions were.

---

### Part 122 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Context:** Chapter 4 — The Fall at Dawn's Rise (v3.3) > Mission 9 — Dawn, Shine at the World's End > The Flame Reaver — Dan Heng's Stand
**Kind:** collective-pointer
**Id:** `chapter-04-fall-at-dawn__the-flame-reaver-dan-heng-s-stand`
**Collective:** `databank/memories/collective/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`
**Witness:** spoke

*(The Flame Reaver corners them in the Chrysos Heirs' baths.)_

**Dan Heng:** ...No. Phainon, take (Trailblazer) to the Vortex of Genesis. I will stay here and hold them off.

**Phainon:** Dan Heng, you...

**Dan Heng:** Don't hesitate, Phainon — you still shoulder the mission of the Era Nova. Amphoreans often speak of "destiny"... The destiny of the Trailblaze is to bind the life and death of every world to our own existence.

> *(Trailblazer)* Such a hasty farewell... I won't allow it.
> **Dan Heng:** This isn't farewell. Don't worry. I'll do everything in my power to survive.

*(Dan Heng transforms into his Imbibitor Lunae form to face the Flame Reaver.)*

**Dan Heng (Imbibitor Lunae):** Even if I were just a passing traveler, I would not hesitate to lay down my life and protect the unfamiliar world beneath my feet... This is the destined course that a Trailblazer cannot escape. I am a Nameless, and what I'm going to do... is to defend all the hopes that are on the verge of fading away.

---
